





\section{MTP Extension of the DataAssemblySet}
\label{sec:AnhangDataAssemblySet}

This chapter specifies all identified extensions of the \emph{DataAssemblySet} and integrates them into the existing MTP specification \cite{PNO.2025.Part3}.

\subsection{Ãœbersicht}
\label{subsec:AnhangDataAssemblySetUebersicht}

\subsubsection{Extension of the IndicatorElements}

According to Chapters~\ref{sec:Reportwerte} and \ref{sec:LeaHmi}, the two interface definitions \emph{SUC StructView} and \emph{SUC ArrayView} are required for value displays in LEA HMIs and for mapping report values on LEA services. According to Section~\ref{sec:Prozesswerte}, \emph{StructView} is also required for process-value outputs of structured data types. As shown in Figure~\ref{fig:ErweiterungIndicatorElement}, \emph{SUC StructView} and \emph{SUC ArrayView}, together with all other interface definitions for report values, are derived from the interface definition \emph{SUC IndicatorElement} according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part3} and organized in \emph{SUCL MTPDataAssemblySUCLib}. The detailed specification is provided in Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangDataAssemblySetSchnittstellen}. This extension, as a result of this dissertation, has already been adopted into the profile \emph{ModuleTypePackage:DataAssemblySet.ComplexTypes V2.0.0} of the MTP specification \cite{PNO.2025.Part3}.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=0.55\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_LEA/IndicatorElement/Klassendiagramm.drawio.png}}
	\caption{Extension of the \emph{DataAssemblySet} for Implementing Structured and Array-Based \emph{IndicatorElements}}
	\label{fig:ErweiterungIndicatorElement}
\end{figure}

\subsubsection{Extension of the OperationElements}

According to Section~\ref{sec:LeaHmi}, the new interface definitions \emph{SUC StructMan}, \emph{SUC StructManInt}, \emph{SUC ArrayMan}, and \emph{SUC ArrayManInt} are required for operator-driven value manipulation in LEA HMIs. As shown in Figure~\ref{fig:ErweiterungOperationElement}, \emph{SUC StructMan} and \emph{SUC ArrayMan}, together with all other interface definitions for value manipulation, are derived from the interface definition \emph{SUC OperationElement} according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part3}. \emph{SUC StructManInt} is derived from \emph{SUC StructMan}, and \emph{SUC ArrayManInt} from \emph{SUC ArrayMan}. All four interface definitions are organized in \emph{SUCL MTPDataAssemblySUCLib}. The detailed specification is provided in Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangDataAssemblySetSchnittstellen}. This extension, as a result of this dissertation, has already been adopted into the profile \emph{ModuleTypePackage:DataAssemblySet.ComplexTypes V2.0.0} of the MTP specification \cite{PNO.2025.Part3}.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=0.8\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_LEA/OperationElement/Klassendiagramm.drawio.png}}
	\caption{Extension of the \emph{DataAssemblySet} for Implementing Structured and Array-Based \emph{OperationElements}}
	\label{fig:ErweiterungOperationElement}
\end{figure}

\subsubsection{Extension of DINT-Based Interfaces with Time Formats}

According to Section~\ref{subsec:SchnittstelleTransportdienst}, report values in a time format are required for the timestamps of a transport service. For this purpose, \emph{RC HasTimeFormat} is introduced. As shown in Figure~\ref{fig:ErweiterungTimeFormat}, this RC can be added as an SRC to all DINT-based interface definitions, in particular to \emph{SUC DIntView}, \emph{SUC DIntMan}, \emph{SUC DIntServParam}, and \emph{SUC DIntProcessValueIn}. \emph{RC HasTimeFormat} is organized in the newly introduced \emph{RCL MTPDataAssemblyRCLib}. The detailed specification is provided in Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangDataAssemblySetSchnittstellen}. 

\emph{RC HasTimeFormat} uses a new \emph{AT TimeFormatAttributeType} to specify the time format in which a DINT value is to be interpreted. As shown in Figure~\ref{fig:ErweiterungTimeFormat}, this AT is organized in \emph{ATL MTPDataAssemblyATLib}. The detailed specification is provided in Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangDataAssemblySetModelle}.

These extensions are assigned to the newly introduced profile \emph{ModuleTypePackage:DataAssemblySet.Time V2.0.0}.\footnote{It is recommended to incorporate \emph{RC HasTimeFormat} and the associated \emph{AT TimeFormatAttributeType} into the base profile \emph{ModuleTypePackage:DataAssemblySet.Base} in the future.}

\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=0.9\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_LEA/Zeitformate/Klassendiagramm.drawio.png}}
	\caption{Extension of the \emph{DataAssemblySet} for Interpreting DINT Values in a Time Format}
	\label{fig:ErweiterungTimeFormat}
\end{figure}

The distinctive feature of this interface definition is the use of a user-defined structured data type. Figure~\ref{fig:CustomDatatypeModellierung} shows how such a data type can be modeled. For this purpose, the rules for modeling complex data types from \cite{PNO.2025.Part5.1} are applied.

%TODO @Format: Bild schÃ¤rfer machen!
\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=1.0\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_LEA/Parameter/Modelling_Custom_Datatype.png}}
	\caption{Modeling of a User-Defined Data Type}
	\label{fig:CustomDatatypeModellierung}
\end{figure}

The complex data type used must be derived from \emph{AT StructuredDataType} defined in \cite{PNO.2025.Part5.1}. When this interface is used, a user-defined ATL must be created, here: CompanyAAttributeLib. Within this ATL, the structured data type that is later to be used in the IE of \emph{SUC StructView} must be specified. By assigning this user-defined AT to the attribute \emph{VType} of \emph{SUC StructView}, the structured data type used is defined. This data type is then expected in the variable \emph{V}. 

\textbf{Note:} If the \emph{StructView} interface is used as a report value, it can be \enquote{frozen} by the variable \emph{ReportValueFreeze} at the \emph{ServiceControl} interface according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}. Optionally, it can be extended by a \emph{MissedValue} variable by assigning \emph{RC MissedValueFlag}.

The challenge of this interface definition is that it must access an array inside the LEA that may have an arbitrary length. In common automation solutions, this is often impossible or possible only under certain conditions because of predefined types. Therefore, a multiplexing mechanism is used that enables access to an array of arbitrary length via a structurally static interface. 

By means of the \emph{OSLevel} variable, it can be defined according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part3} whether the interface is currently operated by the LOL or locally at the LEA. The variable \emph{IndexSel} selects the array position to be displayed, similar to a pointer. The variables \emph{IndexMin} and \emph{IndexMax} specify the lower and upper limits of the array. The variable \emph{IndexCur} specifies the currently selected index. The value of the array at this position is displayed in \emph{V}. 

\emph{VType} defines the data type shared by all array elements. This may be a primitive data type or a user-defined structured data type according to the description of \emph{SUC StructView}.

\textbf{Note 1:} If the \emph{ArrayView} interface is used as a report value, it can be \enquote{frozen} by the variable \emph{ReportValueFreeze} at the \emph{ServiceControl} interface according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}. In this case, the entire array must be frozen, not only the currently selected value. Individual frozen values of the array can then be displayed by selecting the indices. Optionally, the \emph{ArrayView} interface can be extended by a \emph{MissedValue} variable by assigning \emph{RC MissedValueFlag}. 

\textbf{Note 2:} If the \emph{ArrayView} interface is used as a report value and several or all values of an array are to be read for documentation purposes, several or all indices between \emph{IndexMin} and \emph{IndexMax} must be entered successively by the LOL at the \emph{ArrayView} interface. The values of the individual array elements can then be stored one after another. This must also work in the frozen state.

\emph{VMan} is used to enter the desired value of the variable. Analogous to the concept described in \cite{PNO.2025.Part3}, \emph{VRbk} is used to verify the communication between an LOL and the \emph{StructMan} interface within an LEA and indicates the raw value communicated to the LEA. \emph{VOut} indicates the value passed to a further LEA-internal block, possibly with applied constraints. The variable \emph{VFbk} is used to display the current value of the structure influenced by the \emph{StructMan} interface. 

The distinctive feature of this interface definition is the use of a user-defined structured data type. The modeling and use of such a type have already been described in connection with \emph{SUC StructView} and are applied in the same way for \emph{SUC StructMan}. This data type is then expected behind the variables \emph{VOut}, \emph{VMan}, \emph{VRbk}, and \emph{VFbk}.

The \emph{StructManInt} interface extends the \emph{StructMan} interface by internal value specification and a \emph{SourceMode} according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part3}. If the internal access channel is selected, an internal LEA value is used instead of the external value specification. Otherwise, the function of this interface is identical to that of the \emph{StructMan} interface.

		\\ \hline

As already described for the \emph{ArrayView} interface, the challenge of this interface lies in accessing an array within an LEA that may have an arbitrary length. As described in the context of \emph{SUC ArrayView}, access to this array is also index-based in the case of the \emph{ArrayMan} interface.

The array position to be modified is selected via the variable \emph{IndexSel}. The variables \emph{IndexMin} and \emph{IndexMax} specify the lower and upper limits of the array. The variable \emph{IndexCur} specifies the currently selected index of the variable to be manipulated. The variable \emph{VMan} is used to enter the desired value for the variable to be manipulated. Analogous to the concept specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part3}, \emph{VRbk} is used to verify the communication between an LOL and the \emph{ArrayMan} interface within an LEA and indicates the raw value of the variable communicated to the LEA. When a new index is selected, the variables \emph{VMan} and \emph{VRbk} are set to the value at the selected position in the array. \emph{VOut} indicates the value passed to a further LEA-internal block, possibly with limitations. The variable \emph{VFbk} is used to display the current value of the structure affected by the \emph{ArrayMan} interface. \emph{VType} defines the data type shared by all array elements. This may be a primitive data type or a user-defined structured data type.

The \emph{ArrayManInt} interface extends the \emph{ArrayMan} interface by internal value specification and a \emph{SourceMode} according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part3}. If the internal access channel is selected, an internal LEA value is used instead of the external value specification. Otherwise, the function of this interface is identical to that of the \emph{ArrayMan} interface.

\subsubsection{Specification of the Role Class HasTimeFormat}

\emph{RC HasTimeFormat} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblyRcHasTimeFormat}) indicates that a DINT-based interface is to be interpreted in a time format. This RC can be assigned as an SRC to the interface definitions \emph{SUC DIntView}, \emph{SUC DIntMan}, \emph{SUC DIntServParam}, and \emph{SUC DIntProcessValueIn}. \emph{RC HasTimeFormat} provides different formats for interpreting DINT values as time values, encoded in the variable \emph{TimeFormat}. The meaning of the values of this variable is shown in Table~\ref{tab:Zeitformate}.

\subsubsection{Extension of the System Unit Class DIntView}

\emph{SUC DIntView} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblySucDIntView}) is used to display DINT values of an LEA. This interface definition is already specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part3} and is extended in this dissertation by the capability to annotate \emph{RC HasTimeFormat} as an SRC.\footnote{Since only the extension of \emph{SUC DIntView} is used in this dissertation, only this case is described here. \emph{SUC DIntMan}, \emph{SUC DIntServParam}, and \emph{SUC DIntProcessValueIn} must be extended in the same way by assigning \emph{RC HasTimeFormat}.}

\section{MTP Extension of the ServiceSet}
\label{sec:AnhangServiceSet}

This chapter specifies all identified extensions of the \emph{ServiceSet} and integrates them into the existing MTP specification \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}.

\subsection{Ãœbersicht}
\label{subsec:AnhangServiceSetUebersicht}

\subsubsection{Semantic Description of LEA Services}

To distinguish the CES and SES procedures introduced in Section~\ref{sec:LeaDienste} for an LOL, a semantic identifier in the form of \emph{FunctionClassificationAttributes} is added to them. Tables~\ref{tab:FunctionClassificationCes} and \ref{tab:FunctionClassificationSes} specify the corresponding \emph{FunctionClassificationAttributes}. \enquote{2.0} denotes the version in major-minor format and can be incremented accordingly when changes are made to the \emph{FunctionClassificationAttributes}.

\subsubsection{Semantic Description of Service Parameters}

To enable a semantic description of service parameters, the model definition \emph{ServiceParameter} is extended by the capability to append \emph{FunctionClassificationAttributes}. The detailed specification is provided in Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangServiceSetModelle}. This extension, as a result of this dissertation, has already been adopted into the base profile \emph{ModuleTypePackage:ServiceSet.Base V2.0.0} of the MTP specification \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}.

Tables~\ref{tab:FunctionClassificationProductId} to \ref{tab:FunctionClassificationPackagingDataSet} specify \emph{FunctionClassificationAttributes} for the parameters \emph{ProductId}, \emph{LogisticsObjectStatus}, \emph{ProductDataSet}, \emph{PackagingId}, and \emph{PackagingDataSet} introduced in Section~\ref{subsec:Parametrierngsmech}. \enquote{2.0} denotes the version in major-minor format and can be incremented accordingly when changes are made to the \emph{FunctionClassificationAttributes}.

\subsubsection{Extension of the ParameterElements}

According to Section~\ref{sec:LeaParameter}, the two new interface definitions \emph{SUC StructServParam} and \emph{SUC ArrayServParam} are required for the parameterization of LEA services. As shown in Figure~\ref{fig:ErweiterungParam}, \emph{SUC StructServParam} and \emph{SUC ArrayServParam}, together with all other interface definitions for service parameters, are derived from the interface definition \emph{SUC ParameterElement} according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part4} and organized in \emph{SUCL MTPDataAssemblySUCLib}. The detailed specification is provided in Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangServiceSetSchnittstellen}. This extension, as a result of this dissertation, has already been adopted into the profile \emph{ModuleTypePackage:ServiceSet.ComplexTypes V2.0.0} of the MTP specification \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=0.55\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_LEA/Parameter/Klassendiagramm.drawio.png}}
	\caption{Extension of the \emph{ServiceSet} for Implementing Structured and Array-Based Service Parameters}
	\label{fig:ErweiterungParam}
\end{figure}
 
\subsubsection{Specification of the LogisticsInteraction}
\label{subsec:LogisticsInteraction}

Section~\ref{sec:LeaParameter} and Section~\ref{subsec:ErmittlungNextNode} introduce the LEA requests to an LOL shown in Table~\ref{tab:ArtenLogisticsInteraction}.

% Arten der LogisticsInteraction
\begin{footnotesize}
	\begin{longtable}{|
		>{\columncolor[HTML]{E0E0E0}}l
		>{\columncolor[HTML]{FFFFFF}}l |}

		\label{tab:ArtenLogisticsInteraction}\\

		\\ \hline

		\multicolumn{1}{|L{3.1cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} ProductParameter-Request}        	& 
		\multicolumn{1}{L{11.53cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} Mit dieser Anfrage bezieht eine LEA unter Angabe einer \emph{ProductId} und eines \emph{LogisticsObjectStatus} einen produktspezifischen Parametersatz vom LOL. } 
		\\ \hline

		\multicolumn{1}{|L{3.1cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} Packaging-ParameterRequest}        	& 
		\multicolumn{1}{L{11.53cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} Mit dieser Anfrage bezieht eine LEA unter Angabe einer \emph{PackagingId} einen verpackungsspezifischen Parametersatz vom LOL. } 
		\\ \hline

		\multicolumn{1}{|L{3.1cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} ProductParameter-UpdatedInfo}        	& 
		\multicolumn{1}{L{11.53cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} Mit dieser Anfrage informiert eine LEA den LOL, dass sich der produktspezifische Parametersatz an einem definierten Arrayindex seines \emph{ProductDataSet} geÃ¤ndert hat. } 
		\\ \hline

		\multicolumn{1}{|L{3.1cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} Packaging-ParameterUpdated-Info}        	& 
		\multicolumn{1}{L{11.53cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} Mit dieser Anfrage informiert eine LEA den LOL, dass sich der verpackungsspezifische Parametersatz an einem definierten Arrayindex seines \emph{PackagingDataSet} geÃ¤ndert hat. } 
		\\ \hline

		\multicolumn{1}{|L{3.1cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} TransportNode-Request}        	& 
		\multicolumn{1}{L{11.53cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} Mit dieser Anfrage bezieht eine LEA unter Angabe einer \emph{TransportId} den nÃ¤chsten anzufahrenden Transportknoten fÃ¼r einen Transportauftrag vom LOL. } 
		\\ \hline
		
	\end{longtable}
\end{footnotesize}

These requests are implemented on the basis of service-operator interaction according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part4} and may occur once or not at all in an LEA. If they occur, they always follow the same sequence and always allow the same responses by an LOL. It is therefore appropriate to standardize these concrete service-operator interactions as logistics-specific interactions, hereafter called \emph{LogisticsInteractions}. This allows an LEA MTP to model \underline{whether} the corresponding \emph{LogisticsInteractions} occur, while the structure of the \emph{Questions} and \emph{Answers} is standardized and does not need to be remodeled for every specific LEA type.
 
Figure~\ref{fig:LogisticsInteractionKonzept} shows the interface and model definitions newly introduced for \emph{LogisticsInteraction}.

	\caption{Extension of the \emph{ServiceSet} for Implementing Logistics Interactions}
	\label{fig:LogisticsInteractionKonzept}
\end{figure}

The setting of a parameter of \emph{SUC StructServParam} is performed via the access channels \emph{Automatic Internal}, \emph{Automatic External}, or \emph{Operator} in the same way as for all other service parameters defined in \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}.

The distinctive feature of this interface definition is the use of a user-defined structured data type. The modeling and use of such a type have already been described in connection with \emph{SUC StructView} and are applied in the same way for \emph{SUC StructServParam}. This data type is then expected behind the variables \emph{VExt}, \emph{VInt}, \emph{VOp}, \emph{VReq}, and \emph{VOut}.

As with the \emph{ArrayView} interface definition, the challenge for \emph{SUC ArrayServParam} is to access an array within an LEA that may have an arbitrary length. As introduced in connection with \emph{SUC ArrayView}, access to this array is also index-based in the case of \emph{SUC ArrayServParam}.

The variables \emph{IndexExt}, \emph{IndexInt}, and \emph{IndexOp} are used to select an array element depending on the operating mode. According to the active access channel, one of these three values is transferred to the variable \emph{IndexCur}. The variables of all three access channels are checked to determine whether they lie within the range between \emph{IndexMin} and \emph{IndexMax}. If an index outside this range is set, the last valid index remains active and the \emph{Worst Quality Code (WQC)} is set to \enquote{Out of Specification} according to \cite{NAMURArbeitskreisAK4.1.1.2023}.

According to the value of the variable \emph{IndexCur}, the array element at the corresponding index is selected for processing. It can then be processed according to the parameter-transfer mechanisms specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}. \emph{VOut} always indicates the configured value of the array element located at the position in the array defined by \emph{IndexCur}. This value does not necessarily have to match the parameter value currently used in the LEA.

\emph{VType} defines the data type shared by all array elements. This may be a primitive data type or a user-defined structured data type according to the description of \emph{SUC StructView}.

\subsubsection{Specification of the Role Class LogisticsInteractionExtension}

\emph{RC LogisticsInteractionExtension} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblyRcLogisticsInteractionExtension}) extends the \emph{ServiceControl} interface definition according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part4} by the variables required for logistics interactions. If a \emph{LogisticsInteraction} is provided in the LEA, exactly one \emph{LogisticsInteractionExtension} must be assigned as an SRC to the \emph{ServiceControl} interface; otherwise none.

A \emph{LogisticsInteraction} follows a principle similar to the service-operator interaction described in \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}. However, for the IDs of the questions, \emph{LogisticsQuestionID}, and answers, \emph{LogisticsAnswerID}, values from the DINT range, instead of DWORD, are used, where the value 0 and negative values may also be valid IDs. The value \enquote{-1} indicates that currently no question or answer is pending. By means of \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam1} and \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam2}, analogous to \emph{InteractAddInfo} from \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}, additional information can be attached to a request, for example \emph{ProductId} and \emph{LogisticsObjectStatus} for \emph{ProductParameterRequest}. The variable \emph{LogisticsAnswerTimeout} allows the entry of a time period that specifies how long an LEA should wait for the response of an LOL. After this time has elapsed, the LEA may execute an alternative program flow without the LOL response. Setting the timeout to 0 is interpreted as deactivation of the timeout function.

\subsubsection{Extension of the System Unit Class ServiceControl}

\emph{SUC ServiceControl} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblySucServiceControl}) defines the base class for controlling MTP services. This interface definition was already defined in the MTP specification and is extended in this dissertation by the capability to connect a RoleClass of type \emph{RC LogisticsInteractionExtension} as an SRC.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class LogisticsInteraction}

\emph{SUC LogisticsInteraction} (Table~\ref{tab:SucLogisticsInteraction}) organizes all model definitions required for the logistics interaction between an LEA and an LOL. It is derived from \emph{SUC TextDefinition} specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part1}. \emph{SUC LogisticsInteraction} is linked to the model definition \emph{SUC HasLogisticsInteraction} via a \emph{TextRef}. \emph{SUC LogisticsInteraction} follows a principle similar to \emph{SUC ServiceInteraction} specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}, with the difference that it contains predefined \emph{LogisticsQuestions}, which are specified below.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class LogisticsQuestion}

\emph{SUC LogisticsQuestion} (Table~\ref{tab:SucLogisticsQuestion}) is an abstract class derived from \emph{SUC Text} from \cite{PNO.2025.Part1} and represents a logistics-specific question that an LEA can ask an LOL. Five specific questions have so far been derived from \emph{LogisticsQuestion}: \emph{ProductParameterRequest}, \emph{PackagingParameterRequest}, \emph{ProductParameterUpdatedInfo}, \emph{PackagingParameterUpdatedInfo}, and \emph{TransportNodeRequest}. Each of these questions may occur either not at all or exactly once in an LEA.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class ProductParameterRequest}

\emph{SUC ProductParameterRequest} (Table~\ref{tab:SucProductParameterRequest}) is derived from \emph{SUC LogisticsQuestion} and is used to request product-specific parameter sets from an LOL. In contrast to \emph{SUC Question} specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}, no \emph{Answers} are modeled in the MTP for \emph{SUC ProductParameterRequest}. Instead, a value in the DINT range is expected as the response. Values greater than or equal to 0 specify the index at which the LOL has written the requested parameter set into the \emph{ProductDataSet} of the LEA. The array limits, minimum and maximum index, must not be exceeded or undershot. The value \enquote{-1} indicates that no answer is yet available. The value \enquote{-2} indicates that an error occurred during the request. Other responses are currently neither required nor valid.

The standard sequence of a \emph{ProductParameterRequest} is shown in Figure~\ref{fig:ProductParameterRequest}.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=0.7\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_LEA/Logistics_Interaction/ProductParameterRequest.png}}
	\caption{Sequence of the Logistics Interaction of a ProductParameterRequest}
	\label{fig:ProductParameterRequest}
\end{figure}
 
Via the corresponding \emph{LogisticsQuestionID}, here: \emph{LogisticsQuestionID} $= 1$, the LEA sends a \emph{ProductParameterRequest} to the LOL and transfers \emph{ProductId} as \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam1} and \emph{LogisticsObjectStatus} as \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam2}. The LOL then determines the required parameter set and writes it into the \emph{ProductDataSet} of the LEA via the corresponding \emph{ArrayServParm} interface. If the parameterization is successful, the LOL returns a \emph{LogisticsAnswer\-ID} $>= 0$, here: \emph{LogisticsAnswerID} $= 3$, to the LEA, reflecting the index of the \emph{ProductDateSet} to which the new parameter set was written. Afterwards, \emph{LogisticsQuestionID} and \emph{LogisticsAnswerID} are set to \enquote{$-1$}, indicating that no question and no answer are currently pending. \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam1} and \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam2} are reset.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class PackagingParameterRequest}

\emph{SUC PackagingParameterRequest} (Table~\ref{tab:SucPackagingParameterRequest}) is derived from \emph{SUC LogisticsQuestion} and is used to request packaging-specific parameter sets from an LOL. In contrast to \emph{SUC Question} specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}, no \emph{Answers} are modeled in the MTP for \emph{SUC PackagingParameterRequest}. Instead, a value in the DINT range is expected as the response. Values greater than or equal to 0 specify the index at which the LOL has written the requested parameter set into the \emph{PackagingDataSet} of the LEA. The array limits, minimum and maximum index, must not be exceeded or undershot. The value \enquote{-1} indicates that no answer is yet available. The value \enquote{-2} indicates that an error occurred during the request. Other responses are currently neither required nor valid.

The standard sequence of a \emph{PackagingParameterRequest} is shown in Figure~\ref{fig:PackagingParameterRequest}.
 
\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=0.7\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_LEA/Logistics_Interaction/PackagingParameterRequest.png}}
	\caption{Sequence of the Logistics Interaction of a PackagingParameterRequest}
	\label{fig:PackagingParameterRequest}
\end{figure}

Via the corresponding \emph{LogisticsQuestionID}, here: \emph{LogisticsQuestionID} $= 2$, the LEA sends a \emph{PackagingParameterRequest} to the LOL and transfers \emph{PackagingId} as \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam1}. The LOL then determines the required parameter set and writes it into the \emph{PackagingDataSet} of the LEA via the corresponding \emph{ArrayServParm} interface. If the parameterization is successful, the LOL returns a \emph{LogisticsAnswerID} $>= 0$, here: \emph{LogisticsAnswerID} $= 2$, to the LEA, reflecting the index of the \emph{PackagingDateSet} to which the new parameter set was written. Afterwards, \emph{LogisticsQuestionID} and \emph{LogisticsAnswerID} are set to \enquote{$-1$}, indicating that no question and no answer are currently pending. \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam1} is reset.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class ProductParameterUpdatedInfo}

\emph{SUC ProductParameterUpdatedInfo} (Table~\ref{tab:SucProductParameterUpdatedInfo}) is derived from \emph{SUC LogisticsQuestion} and is used to inform an LOL that a parameter set in the \emph{ProductDataSet} of an LEA has changed. In contrast to \emph{SUC Question} specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}, no \emph{Answers} are modeled in the MTP for \emph{SUC ProductParameterUpdatedInfo}. Instead, the value \enquote{1} is expected as confirmation that the LOL has acknowledged the parameter change. The value \enquote{-1} indicates that no answer is yet available. The value \enquote{-2} indicates that an error occurred during the request. Other responses are currently neither required nor valid.

The standard sequence of a \emph{ProductParameterUpdatedInfo} is shown in Figure~\ref{fig:ProductParameterUpdatedInfo}.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=0.7\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_LEA/Logistics_Interaction/ProductParameterUpdatedInfo.png}}
	\caption{Sequence of the Logistics Interaction of a ProductParameterUpdatedInfo}
	\label{fig:ProductParameterUpdatedInfo}
\end{figure}

Via the corresponding \emph{LogisticsQuestionID}, here: \emph{LogisticsQuestionID} $= 3$, the LEA sends a \emph{ProductParameterUpdatedInfo} to the LOL and transfers the array index, here: array index $= 5$, of the changed parameter set in the \emph{ProductDataSet} as \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam1}. The LOL parameter management then determines whether the corresponding product parameter data set is also to be adapted in the LOL, if necessary by user request, and updates the parameter set if required. The LOL acknowledges the parameter change by sending \emph{LogisticsAnswerID}~$= 1$ to the LEA. Afterwards, \emph{LogisticsQuestionID} and \emph{LogisticsAnswerID} are set to \enquote{$-1$}, indicating that no question and no answer are currently pending. \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam1} is reset.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class TransportNodeRequest}

\emph{SUC TransportNodeRequest} (Table~\ref{tab:SucTransportNodeRequest}) is derived from \emph{SUC LogisticsQuestion} and is used to request the next transport node to be approached from an LOL. In contrast to \emph{SUC Question} specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}, no \emph{Answers} are modeled in the MTP for \emph{SUC TransportNodeRequest}. Instead, a value in the DINT range is expected as the response. Values greater than 0 directly specify the ID of the next transport node to be approached. This eliminates the need for a separate parameter interface to configure the next transport node to be approached. Only values corresponding to the ID of a transport node in the respective MLS may be returned as a response. The value \enquote{0} indicates that the \emph{FinalTargetNode} specified in the transport service interface is to be used as the next transport node. The value \enquote{-1} indicates that no answer is yet available. The value \enquote{-2} indicates that an error occurred during the request. Other responses are currently neither required nor valid.

		\multicolumn{3}{L{11.83cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} (no further constraints given)} 
		\\ \hline

		\multicolumn{1}{|L{2.8cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} Allowed Children}     & 
		\multicolumn{3}{L{11.83cm}|}{\cellcolor[HTML]{FFFFFF} (no further constraints given)} 
		\\ \hline

	\end{longtable}
\end{footnotesize}

The standard sequence of a \emph{TransportNodeRequest} is shown in Figure~\ref{fig:TransportNodeRequest}.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=0.8\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/08_Bereichs-Automatisierung/TransportNodeRequest.png}}
	\caption{Sequence of the Logistics Interaction of a TransportNodeRequest}
	\label{fig:TransportNodeRequest}
\end{figure}

Via the corresponding \emph{LogisticsQuestionID}, here: \emph{LogisticsQuestionID} $= 5$, the LEA sends a \emph{TransportNodeRequest} to the LOL and transfers the \emph{TransportId} of the associated transport service as \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam1}. The LOL then determines the required next transport node. If the next transport node is successfully determined, the LOL returns a \emph{LogisticsAnswerID} $>= 0$ to the LEA. This response directly reflects the ID of the next transport node to be approached. A \emph{LogisticsAnswerID} $= 0$ indicates that the \emph{FinalTargetNode} of the transport service is to be used. Afterwards, \emph{LogisticsQuestionID} and \emph{LogisticsAnswerID} are set to \enquote{$-1$}, indicating that no question or answer is currently pending. \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam1} and \emph{LogisticsQuestionParam2} are reset. The information received about the next transport node is transferred by the LEA to the procedure parameter \emph{NextNode} in the corresponding transport service.

\subsubsection{Specification of the Role Class HasLogisticsInteraction}

\emph{RC HasLogisticsInteraction} (Table~\ref{tab:RcHasLogisticsInteraction}) is derived from \emph{RC HasTextReference} specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part1}. \emph{SUC HasLogisticsInteraction} is used to assign a \emph{LogisticsInteraction} to the model definition \emph{SUC Service}, specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}. For this purpose, a \emph{SUC LogisticsInteraction} model definition is referenced by means of \emph{TextRef}. If a \emph{LogisticsInteraction} is provided in an LEA, exactly one \emph{SUC HasLogisticsInteraction} must be assigned to the LEA service as a RoleRequirement; otherwise none.

\section{MTP Extension of the ProcessValueSet}
\label{sec:AnhangProcessValueSet}

This chapter specifies all identified extensions of the \emph{ProcessValueSet} and integrates them into the existing MTP specification \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}.

\subsection{Ãœbersicht}
\label{subsec:AnhangProcessValueSetUebersicht}

\subsubsection{Extension of the ProcessValueInputs}

According to Section~\ref{sec:Prozesswerte}, the interface definitions \emph{SUC StructProcessValueIn} and \emph{SUC ArrayProcessValueIn} are intended to specify process-value inputs for structured data types and array data types. As shown in Figure~\ref{fig:ErweiterungProcessValueInputs}, \emph{SUC StructProcessValueIn} and \emph{SUC ArrayProcessValueIn}, together with all other interface definitions for process-value inputs, are derived from the interface definition \emph{SUC InputElement} according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part4} and organized in \emph{SUCL MTPDataAssemblySUCLib}. The detailed specification is provided in Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangProcessValueSetSchnittstellen}. This extension, as a result of this dissertation, has already been adopted into the profile \emph{ModuleTypePackage:ProcessValueSet.ComplexTypes V2.0.0} of the MTP specification \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=0.55\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_LEA/Prozesswerte/ProzesswerteingÃ¤nge/Klassendiagramm.drawio.png}}
	\caption{Extension of the \emph{ProcessValueSet} for Implementing Structured and Array-Based Process Value Inputs}
	\label{fig:ErweiterungProcessValueInputs}
\end{figure}

\subsubsection{Extension of the ProcessValueOutputs}

According to Section~\ref{sec:Prozesswerte}, interface definitions for process-value outputs of structured data types and array data types are to be specified. For process-value outputs of structured data types, the associated \emph{IndicatorElement}, i.e. \emph{SUC StructView}, can be used as for all other MTP data types according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}. For array-based process-value outputs, a separate interface definition \emph{SUC ArrayProcessValueOut} is provided because, unlike \emph{SUC ArrayView}, it does not require an \emph{OSLevel} variable for access control. An \emph{ArrayProcessValueOut} interface is always accessed by another LEA and not by an operator or LOL. Following the principles of the MTP concept, \emph{SUC ArrayProcessValueOut} is derived from an abstract \emph{SUC OutputElement}. \emph{SUC OutputElement}, in turn, is derived from \emph{SUC DataAssembly} specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part3}. The newly introduced interface definitions are shown in Figure~\ref{fig:ErweiterungProcessValueOutputs} and organized in \emph{SUCL MTPDataAssemblySUCLib}. The detailed specification is provided in Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangProcessValueSetSchnittstellen}. This extension, as a result of this dissertation, has already been adopted into the profile \emph{ModuleTypePackage:ProcessValueSet.ComplexTypes V2.0.0} of the MTP specification \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=0.55\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_LEA/Prozesswerte/ProzesswertausgÃ¤nge/Klassendiagramm.drawio.png}}
	\caption{Extension of the \emph{ProcessValueSet} for Implementing Structured and Array-Based Process Value Outputs}
	\label{fig:ErweiterungProcessValueOutputs}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Interface Definitions}
\label{subsec:AnhangProcessValueSetSchnittstellen}

The required value is transferred in the variable \emph{V} according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part4}. The distinctive feature of this interface definition is the use of a user-defined structured data type. The modeling and use of such a type have already been described in connection with \emph{SUC StructView} and are applied in the same way for \emph{SUC StructProcessValueIn}. This data type is then expected behind the variable \emph{V}.

Ã„hnlich wie bei der \emph{SUC ArrayView} besteht die Herausforderung bei dieser Schnittstelle darin, auf ein Array innerhalb einer LEA zuzugreifen, das eine beliebige LÃ¤nge haben kann. Wie bei der \emph{SUC ArrayView} soll der Zugriff auf dieses Array auch im Falle der \emph{SUC ArrayProcessValueIn} indexbasiert erfolgen.

The array position to be displayed is selected via the variable \emph{IndexSel}. The variables \emph{IndexMin} and \emph{IndexMax} specify the lower and upper limits of the array. The variable \emph{IndexCur} specifies the currently selected index, and the value of the array at this position is displayed in \emph{V}. \emph{VType} defines the data type shared by all array elements. This may be a primitive data type or a structured data type.

\textbf{Note:} This interface definition differs from all other interfaces derived from the \emph{InputElement} interface definition because it also includes information flows from the LEA to the LOL. This had not previously been envisaged.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class OutputElement}

\emph{SUC OutputElement} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblySucOutputElement}) is an abstract interface from which specific process-value outputs of different data types can be derived. The interface definition itself serves only an organizational purpose and provides a variable for transmitting a \emph{Worst Quality Code (WQC)}.

The interface definition \emph{SUC ArrayProcessValueOut} corresponds to the interface definition \emph{SUC ArrayView}. The only difference is that \emph{SUC ArrayProcessValueOut} does not contain an \emph{OSLevel} variable because it is always controlled by another LEA.


\section{MTP Extension of the ServerAssemblySet}

This chapter specifies all identified extensions of the \emph{ServerAssemblySet} and integrates them into the existing MTP specification \cite{PNO.2025.Part5.1} and \cite{PNO.2025.Part5}, respectively.

\subsubsection{Mapping Complex Data Types in OPC~UA}

The specification chapters~\ref{sec:AnhangDataAssemblySet}, \ref{sec:AnhangServiceSet}, and \ref{sec:AnhangProcessValueSet} described interface definitions with complex data types, Struct and Array. To transmit these data types via OPC~UA, not only the existing primitive data types but also the mapping of complex data types into the address space of the OPC~UA server of an LEA must be specified.

Figure~\ref{fig:StructOPCUA} shows the mapping of variables of \textbf{structured data types} in OPC~UA. 

\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=0.7\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_LEA/Komplexe Datentypen/Komplexe_Typen_OPC_UA.drawio.png}}
	\caption{Mapping of Structured Data Types in OPC~UA Based on \cite{PNO.2025.Part5}}
	\label{fig:StructOPCUA}
\end{figure}

Variables of a structured data type have a \emph{HasTypeDefinition} to an OPC~UA \emph{VariableType} that describes the underlying structure. This \emph{VariableType} has a data type that is an OPC~UA \emph{DataType}. This \emph{DataType}, in turn, has a \emph{StructureDefinition} that contains a list of \emph{StructureFields}, not shown in the figure. These \emph{StructureFields} correspond to the subordinate variables of the \emph{VariableType} and thus to the complex data type to be mapped. This modeling of structured data types is possible with the native OPC~UA means according to \cite{OPCFoundation.10000-3}. As a result of this dissertation, it has already been adopted into the profile \emph{ModuleTypePackage:ServerAssemblySet.OPCUA V2.0.0} of the MTP specification \cite{PNO.2025.Part5}.

Variables with \textbf{array data types} do not require additional rules for mapping to OPC~UA. By using the multiplexing mechanism, the arrays are represented in OPC~UA in the form of primitive or structured data types. 


\section{MTP Specification of the ChoreographySet}
\label{sec:AnhangChoreoAspekt}

This chapter specifies the \emph{ChoreographySet} as a new aspect of the MTP specification that contains all elements identified in the conceptual chapter~\ref{chap:Art2LL}.
 
\subsection{Ãœbersicht}
\label{subsec:AnhangChoreographySetUebersicht}

According to Chapter~\ref{chap:Art2LL}, a series of new model and interface definitions is required to represent choreography-relevant information in the MTP of an LEA. Figure~\ref{fig:AnhangUebersichtChoreoAspekt} provides an overview of these newly specified definitions.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=1.0\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_Choreografie/Klassendiagramm.drawio.png}}
	\caption{Specification of the \emph{ChoreographySet} for Implementing Choreography-Based Logistics Lines}
	\label{fig:AnhangUebersichtChoreoAspekt}
\end{figure}

\subsubsection{Interface Definitions}

On the interface-definition side, \emph{SUC ChoreographyParticipantManager} is introduced as an interface for configuring configurable logic, and \emph{SUC CommunicationManager} is introduced as an interface for configurable communication. \emph{SUC CommunicationManager} is an abstract interface definition that fundamentally allows the use of different communication technologies through different derivations. For implementation based on OPC~UA client/server, the derived \emph{SUC OpcUaClientServerManager} is introduced. A convention in the MTP specifications provides that interface definitions belonging together in terms of content are derived from a common interface definition with the suffix \emph{*Element}. Accordingly, in this case \emph{SUC ChoreographyElement}, derived from \emph{SUC DataAssembly} \cite{PNO.2025.Part3}, is introduced, from which \emph{SUC ChoreographyParticipantManager} and \emph{SUC CommunicationManager} are derived.

For individual values exchanged and processed within a choreography, \emph{SUC UnionElement}, derived from \emph{SUC DataAssembly} \cite{PNO.2025.Part1}, is introduced according to Section~\ref{subsec:UnionType} to display a value with configurable data type. For the communication variant of active writing, \emph{SUC WritableUnionElements}, derived from \emph{SUC UnionElement}, is additionally provided\footnote{This derivation is possible because a write access always includes reading back the written value. A write access is therefore an extension of a read access. } and enables write access to a value with configurable data type. 

These interface definitions are organized in \emph{SUCL MTPDataAssemblySUCLib}. The detailed specification is provided in Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangChoreographySetSchnittstellen}. 

\subsubsection{Model Definitions}

On the model-definition side, \emph{SUC ChoreographySet} is introduced as a new aspect set for organizing all choreography-related models and is derived from the abstract \emph{SUC MTPSet} according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part1}. The \emph{ChoreographySet} always contains exactly one IE of \emph{SUC ChoreographyParticipant}, derived from \emph{SUC LinkedObject} \cite{PNO.2025.Part1}. It indicates that an LEA has the capability to participate in a choreography and is linked to a \emph{ChoreographyParticipantManager} interface. The input list and output list of the choreography participant are represented by the subordinate \emph{SUC InputList} and \emph{SUC OutputList}. These lists can contain any number of IEs of \emph{SUC InputElemente} and \emph{SUC OutputElement}. They are derived from \emph{SUC LinkedObject} and represent incoming or outgoing system variables. According to Section~\ref{subsec:ChoreoKonfiguration}, the classes \emph{SUC FixedInputElement}, \emph{SUC ConfigurableInputElement}, \emph{SUC WritableInputElement}, \emph{SUC FixedOutputElement}, and \emph{SUC ConfigurableOutputElement} are provided as concrete derivations of \emph{SUC InputElemente} and \emph{SUC OutputElement}. Almost all \emph{InputElements} and \emph{OutputElements} are linked to a \emph{UnionElement} interface via LinkedObject relations. An exception is formed by \emph{WritableInputElements}, each of which is assigned to a \emph{WritableUnionElement} interface. In the case of \emph{ConfigurableInputElements} and \emph{ConfigurableOutpuElements}, an ID link relation to a \emph{CommunicationManager} interface also exists, which handles value transfer in the sense of configurable communication. The model definitions are organized in the newly introduced library \emph{SUCL MTPChoreographySUCLib}. The detailed specification is provided in Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangChoreographySetModelle}.

All model and interface definitions required for the \emph{ChoreographySet} are assigned to the new profile \emph{ModuleTypePackage:ChoreographySet.Base V2.0.0}.\footnote{\emph{SUC CommunicationManager} and the derived \emph{SUC OpcUaClientServerManager} can in principle also be used for configurable communication independently of choreographies, for example in decentralized orchestrations. Since such approaches are not yet provided in the MTP specification, these interface definitions are initially assigned to the \emph{ChoreographySet}. For future cross-cutting use cases, a shift into the \emph{ServerAssemblySet} \cite{PNO.2025.Part5.1} may be appropriate. }

\subsection{Interface Definitions}
\label{subsec:AnhangChoreographySetSchnittstellen}

The variables \emph{VReal}, \emph{VDInt}, \emph{VDWord}, \emph{VBool}, and \emph{VString } are used to display the desired value. The variable \emph{DataType} indicates which data type is currently active and which of the previously mentioned variables is therefore to be interpreted. \emph{UnionElement} can thus display only one value of one defined data type at a time. \emph{VQC} provides information about the quality and trustworthiness of the displayed value.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class WritableUnionElement}

\emph{SUC WritableUnionElement} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblySucWritableUnionElement}) is derived from \emph{UnionElement} and is used to write a value into a \emph{WritableInputElement}. Accordingly, a \emph{WritableUnionElement} interface is always assigned to a \emph{WritableInputElement} via a LinkedObject relation.

\end{footnotesize}

The variables \emph{VReal}, \emph{VDInt}, \emph{VDWord}, \emph{VBool}, and \emph{VString} are used to enter the desired value. The variable \emph{DataType} indicates which data type is currently active and which of the previously mentioned variables is to be used in the LEA program. \emph{WritableUnionElement} thus accepts only one value of one defined data type at a time. \emph{VQC} can be used to transmit information about the quality and trustworthiness of the entered value.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class ChoreographyElement}

\emph{SUC ChoreographyElement} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblySucChoreographyElement}) is an abstract class derived from \emph{SUC DataAssembly} specified in \cite{PNO.2025.Part3}. The choreography-relevant interface definitions \emph{ChoreographyParticipantManager} and \emph{CommunicationManager} are derived from \emph{ChoreographyElement}.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class ChoreographyParticipantManager}

\emph{SUC ChoreographyParticipantManager} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblySucChoreographyParticipantManager}) is derived from \emph{SUC ChoreographyElement} and is used to configure the configurable logic of a choreography participant. In addition, it provides information for type, version, and instance verification of choreographed logistics lines, see also Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangManifestWorkflows}. This interface definition is assigned to an \emph{SUC ChoreographyParticipant} in the \emph{ChoreographySet} via a LinkedObject relation.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class CommunicationManager}

\emph{SUC CommunicationManager} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblySucCommunicationManager}) is an abstract class derived from \emph{SUC ChoreographyElement}. It is to be understood as a generic interface definition for configuring the configurable communication of a choreography participant. To use this interface definition, a concrete manager for a specific communication technology must be derived from it. So far, only \emph{OpcUaClientServerManager} has been implemented for configuring OPC~UA client/server connections; additional derivations can be developed in the future. The derivations of \emph{SUC CommunicationManager} are assigned to the model definitions of \emph{SUC ConfigurableInputElements} and \emph{SUC ConfigurableOutputElements} in the \emph{ChoreographySet} via an ID link relation, variable \emph{ManagerLink}. In addition, each model definition specifies a \emph{ManagerIndex} that refers to a concrete communication element within the \emph{CommunicationManager}.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class OpcUaClientServerManager}

\emph{SUC OpcUaClientServerManager} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblySucOpcUaClientServerManager}) is derived from the abstract \emph{SUC CommunicationManager}. It is used to configure OPC~UA client/server communication of an LEA with other LEAs participating in a choreography. \emph{SUC OpcUaClientServerManager} is assigned to the model definitions of \emph{SUC ConfigurableInputElements} and \emph{SUC ConfigurableOutputElements} in the \emph{ChoreographySet} via an ID link relation, variable \emph{ManagerLink}. Each model definition specifies a \emph{ManagerIndex} that refers to a concrete communication element within the \emph{CommunicationManager}. In the case of \emph{OpcUaClientServerManager}, these communication elements are the \emph{UaReader} and \emph{UaWriter} managed by the manager, which are referenced via their index. The \emph{UaReader} are each assigned to a \emph{ConfigurableInputElement}, and the \emph{UaWriter} are each assigned to a \emph{ConfigurableOutputElement}. For the communication variant of active writing, \emph{SUC OpcUaClientServerManager} manages the existing \emph{ValueFields} of an LEA that can be written by other LEAs.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class ChoreographyParticipant}

\emph{SUC ChoreographyParticipant} (Table~\ref{tab:SucChoreographyParticipant}) describes an LEA as a choreography participant. The interface definition \emph{SUC ChoreographyParticipantManager} is assigned to this model definition via a LinkedObject relation.

% Modelldefinition SUC ChoreographyParticipant

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class InputElement}

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class FixedInputElement}

\emph{SUC FixedInputElement} (Table~\ref{tab:SucFixedInputElement}) is derived from \emph{SUC InputElement} and describes a statically defined incoming system variable provided by the choreography participant itself. A \emph{FixedInputElement} is assigned to a \emph{UnionElement} interface via a LinkedObject relation.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class ConfigurableInputElement}

\emph{SUC ConfigurableInputElement} (Table~\ref{tab:SucConfigurableInputElement}) is derived from \emph{SUC InputElement} and describes a configurable incoming system variable received by the choreography participant from another choreography participant. A \emph{ConfigurableInputElement} is assigned to a derivation of the \emph{CommunicationManager} interface via an ID link relation using the variable \emph{ManagerLink}. A \emph{ManagerIndex} is used to refer to a specific communication element within the \emph{CommunicationManager}. In this way, the communication is configured such that the required system variable is exchanged. The interpretation of \emph{ManagerIndex} depends on the derivation of \emph{CommunicationManager} used. In the case of \emph{OpcUaClientServerManager}, this is the index of the reader used.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class WritableInputElement}

\emph{SUC WritableInputElement} (Table~\ref{tab:SucWritableInputElement}) is derived from \emph{SUC InputElement} and describes an incoming system variable into which values can be written by another choreography participant. A \emph{WritableInputElement} is assigned to a \emph{WritableUnionElement} interface definition via a LinkedObject relation.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class OutputElement}

\emph{SUC OutputElement} (Table~\ref{tab:SucOutputElement}) describes an outgoing system variable relevant to the configurable logic of a choreography participant. This may be a statically defined internal process variable of the participant or a configurable process variable received by the participant from another participant. An \emph{OutputElement} is always assigned to a \emph{UnionElement} interface via a LinkedObject relation.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class ConfigurableOutputElement}

\emph{SUC ConfigurableOutputElement} (Table~\ref{tab:SucConfigurableOutputElement}) is derived from \emph{SUC OutputElement} and describes a configurable outgoing system variable sent by the choreography participant to another choreography participant. A \emph{ConfigurableOutputElement} is assigned to a derivation of the \emph{CommunicationManager} interface via an ID link relation using the variable \emph{ManagerLink}. A \emph{ManagerIndex} is used to refer to a specific communication element within the \emph{CommunicationManager}. In this way, the communication is configured such that the required system variable is exchanged. The interpretation of \emph{ManagerIndex} depends on the derivation of \emph{CommunicationManager} used. In the case of \emph{OpcUaClientServerManager}, this is the index of the writer used.

\section{MTP Specification of the TransportSet}
\label{sec:AnhangTransportAspekt}

This chapter specifies the \emph{TransportSet} as a new aspect of the MTP specification that contains all elements identified in the conceptual chapter~\ref{chap:Art3LA}.

\subsection{Ãœbersicht}
\label{subsec:AnhangTransportSetUebersicht}

\subsubsection{Semantic Description of Transport Services}

For semantic identification of the transport services introduced in Section~\ref{sec:Transportdienste}, a semantic identifier in the form of a \emph{FunctionClassificationAttribute} is added to them. Table~\ref{tab:FunctionClassificationTransportdienst} specifies the corresponding \emph{FunctionClassificationAttribute}. \enquote{2.0} denotes the version in major-minor format and can be incremented accordingly when changes are made to the \emph{FunctionClassificationAttributes}.

\subsubsection{Specification of the Transport Aspect}

According to Chapter~\ref{chap:Art3LA}, a series of new model and interface definitions is required to represent transport-relevant information in the MTP of an LEA. Figure~\ref{fig:AnhangUebersichtTransportAspekt} provides an overview of these newly specified definitions.
 
\begin{figure}[htbp]
	\centerline{\includegraphics[width=1.0\textwidth]{Inhalt/Abbildungen/99_Anhang/Spezifikation_Transport/Klassendiagramm.drawio.png}}
	\caption{Specification of the \emph{TransportSet} for Connecting Flexible Transport Systems to LEAs}
	\label{fig:AnhangUebersichtTransportAspekt}
\end{figure}

On the \textbf{interface-definition} side, \emph{SUC TransportClientManager} is introduced as an interface definition for configuring and establishing a communication link between an LEA and transport management. It is an abstract interface definition that fundamentally allows the use of different communication technologies through different derivations. For implementation based on OPC~UA client/server, the derived \emph{SUC OpcUaTransportClientManager} is introduced. In addition, \emph{SUC TransportNodeManager} is introduced as an interface that enables the assignment of a transport node of an LEA to the associated TN proxy in transport management. A convention in the MTP specifications provides that interface definitions belonging together in terms of content are derived from a common interface definition with the suffix \emph{*Element}. Accordingly, in this case \emph{SUC TransportElement}, derived from \emph{SUC DataAssembly} \cite{PNO.2025.Part3}, is introduced, from which \emph{SUC TransportClientManager} and \emph{SUC TransportNodeManager} are derived. These interface definitions are organized in \emph{SUCL MTPDataAssemblySUCLib}. The detailed specification is provided in Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangTransportSetSchnittstellen}. 

On the \textbf{model-definition} side, \emph{SUC TransportSet} is introduced as a new aspect set for organizing all transport-relevant models and is derived from the abstract \emph{SUC MTPSet} according to \cite{PNO.2025.Part1}. The \emph{TransportSet} indicates that an LEA has the capability to be connected to a flexible transport system according to the concepts of this dissertation and contains all model definitions required for this purpose. In particular, this consists of any number of IEs of \emph{SUC TransportNode}. The latter is an abstract class for representing transport nodes and is derived from \emph{SUC LinkedObject}. The concrete derivations provided are \emph{SUC InboundNode}, \emph{SUC OutboundNode}, \emph{SUC InOutboundNode}, \emph{SUC ProcessingNode}, and \emph{SUC OrderNode}. All \emph{TransportNodes} are linked to one \emph{TransportClientManager} interface each by means of an ID link relation and to one \emph{TransportNodeManager} interface by means of LinkedObject relations. The model definitions are organized in the newly introduced library \emph{SUCL MTPTransportSUCLib}. The detailed specification is provided in Appendix~\ref{subsec:AnhangTransportSetModelle}. 

All model and interface definitions required for the \emph{TransportSet} are assigned to the new profile \emph{ModuleTypePackage:TransportSet.Base V2.0.0}.
 
\subsection{Interface Definitions}
\label{subsec:AnhangTransportSetSchnittstellen}

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class TransportClientManager}

\emph{SUC TransportClientManager} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblySucTransportClientManager}) is derived from \emph{SUC TransportElement} and is an abstract interface definition for configuring the communication link between an LEA and a flexible transport system. To implement this interface definition, a concrete manager must be derived from it. So far, only \emph{SUC OpcUaTransportClientManager} has been specified as a derivation. \emph{SUC TransportClientManager}, and thus also its derivations, are assigned to a \emph{TransportNode} model definition in the \emph{TransportSet} via an ID link relation.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class OpcUaTransportClientManager}

\emph{SUC OpcUaTransportClientManager} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblySucOpcUaTransportClientManager}) is derived from \emph{SUC TransportClientManager} and is used to configure and establish an OPC~UA client/server communication link between the LEA and a flexible transport system. In addition, this interface contains the variable \emph{LeaStateCur}, which enables transport management to determine the state of the LEA service. This is used to detect possible faults in the LEA and, if necessary, reroute transport services to this LEA.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class TransportNodeManager}

\emph{SUC TransportNodeManager} (Table~\ref{tab:DataAssemblySucTransportNodeManager}) is derived from \emph{SUC TransportElement} and is used to assign a TN proxy to a specific transport node in the LEA. This interface definition is assigned to a \emph{TransportNode} model definition in the \emph{TransportSet} via a LinkedObject relation.

\subsubsection{Specification of the System Unit Class TransportNode}

\emph{SUC TransportNode} (Table~\ref{tab:SucTransportNode}) is an abstract model definition for describing a transport node available in an LEA. Currently, five concrete types of transport nodes are derived from this model definition: \emph{SUC InboundNode}, \emph{SUC OutboundNode}, \emph{SUC InOutboundNode}, \emph{SUC ProcessingNode}, and \emph{SUC OrderNode}. A \emph{SUC TransportNode} is assigned to the \emph{TransportNodeManager} interface definition via a LinkedObject relation, which enables the assignment of the transport node to a TN proxy in transport management. In addition, \emph{SUC TransportNode} is assigned to the \emph{TransportClientManager} interface, which connects the LEA to transport management. For this assignment, the ID link mechanism and the variable \emph{ClientLink} are used.

\section{Conformity Declaration for Logistics Equipment Assemblies}
\label{sec:KonformitÃ¤tsbeschreibung}

Based on the findings of this dissertation, Table~\ref{tab:KonformitÃ¤tsbeschreibung} provides an overview of the existing and newly introduced profiles required for applying the MTP concept in production-related logistics. A distinction is made between profiles that are generally relevant for LEA automation, profiles that LEAs must fulfill in order to participate in a logistics line, and profiles required for connecting LEAs to flexible transport systems.

It should be noted with regard to this list that this dissertation initially considers the core aspects of Parts 1 to 5 of the MTP specification. Logistics-specific extensions may also arise in further aspects of the MTP specification in the future. For example, it may become necessary to provide alarms at LEA level rather than at service or individual-control level. In addition, logistics-specific diagnostic functions may be required. These aspects should be investigated in future work, see also Chapter~\ref{chap:ausblick}.
